#2370. Feyenoord second best to Betis as Isco is on the spot for CL win

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord endured a frustrating evening in Spain as a penalty by Real Betis’s outstanding player, Isco, secured a 1-0 win for the La Liga side.

The Rotterdammers were second best for most of the game, but held out until the 59th minute, when Abde Ezzalzouli’s cross hit the arm of Jeremiah St. Juste in front of goal.

Isco, who was at the heart of Betis’s attacks all evening, grabbed the ball and gave Tjark Ernst no chance from the penalty spot.

Ernst had made good saves from former AZ Alkmaar striker Troy Parrott and Marc Batra to keep Feyenoord in the game in the first half, but Feyenoord had the best chance of the opening period when Gjivai Zechiël shot over the bar from close range.

The visitors had a claim for a penalty of their own turned down in the 68th minute when Givairo Read claimed he had been pushed by Ezzalzouli, but referee Simone Sozza waved the appeals away.

The result leaves Giovanni van Bronckhorst’s side in 26th place in the Champions League table, with Internazionale up next in De Kuip on November 3.

#2366. Klopp quick to shut down Havertz criticism: "He's a top player"

Jürgen Klopp has jumped to Kai Havertz's defence after some underwhelming displays as Germany's starting striker.

“I've played against Kai many times, and I can tell you that preparing for a game against him is no walk in the park.

“You get a real sense of just how complete a player he is,” Klopp said, reflecting on his time as Liverpool coach when facing Arsenal.

"You always have to be prepared for him to produce an outstanding performance because he has all the qualities to do so.

"He's a top player. Mikel Arteta is someone who, as a person and as a coach, is absolutely determined to win.

"And unless several negative scenarios all come together, Kai Havertz starts games for Arsenal.

“The fact that he plays here is not simply because I don't want to leave him out, but because I want to win games,” Klopp added.

The DFB head coach said the 27-year-old was unlucky not to find the net against Serbia. Klopp also senses the potential Havertz can bring to Germany's attack going forward.

"Take the game against Serbia, for example. Kai put in an outstanding performance. He could have scored two goals and, under certain circumstances, perhaps even should have.

"What interests me is his potential and what we can achieve together with him going forward, rather than dwelling on what has happened so far.

"I've watched Kai Havertz – I wouldn't claim to have seen every single international match, but whenever Kai Havertz has been on my radar, I've considered him an absolute top player.

“So I'm really looking forward to working with him. And if there's anything more we can get out of him, I'm pretty confident that we'll manage to do that together,” he concluded.

Having failed to score against Serbia last week, Havertz has only netted once for Germany in his last five appearances.

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord preserved their four-point advantage over champions PSV after coming away with a point from Eindhoven thanks to an improved second-half performance.

PSV dominated the first half as they deployed a close-passing, tiki-taka style to stifle Feyenoord, but only had Guus Til’s well-taken volley after 20 minutes to show for it.

Ten minutes after the break Feyenoord equalised when Anis Hadj Moussa was given too much space to charge down the right flank and bend a shot inside the far post.

The Algerian had a good chance for a second towards the end, while PSV goalkeeper Matej Kovar had to be alert to keep out a header by Nacho Ferri from close range.

“We were by far the better team today and had the better chances,” said PSV coach Peter Bosz. “We started the second half badly and made a mistake, after which Hadj Moussa scored. And even then we could have won it.”

Ajax overtook PSV to close the gap at the top to three points with a hard-fought win at Sparta. Veteran striker Jens Toornstra scored for Sparta on his 500th Eredivisie appearance, but Abdellah Ouazane and Tolu Arokodare, the scorers against Atalanta in midweek, were on target again to claim the win for Ajax.

Twente moved up to fourth place as two late goals by Sondre Ørjasæter and Lucas Vennegoor of Hesselink earned a 2-0 win at AZ Alkmaar, who like Twente and PSV have 20 points.

Telstar lost by the same margin at Go Ahead Eagles, whose striker Søren Tengstedt netted twice in the first half to lead the Eredivisie scoring charts with eight goals.

#2362. Sports club members becoming less tolerant of homosexuality

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Acceptance of homosexuality in Dutch sports clubs has declined significantly in the last generation, according to a survey of attitudes by the Mulier Institute.

The organisation found that 66% of people had no objection to a fellow member of a sports club being gay, while in 2008 the figure was 85%.

Just over half (55%) of respondents said they had no problem sharing a shower with a gay team-mate of the same sex, which also represented a drop from 73% in 2008.

The Mulier Institute said the findings reflected a social trend that has also been seen in schools, where the proportion of boys aged between 12 and 16 with a positive view of homosexuality has dropped from 40% to 22% since 2021.

The Dutch Olympic Committee NOC*NSF said the findings were “highly regrettable” and pledged to step up its efforts to make sport more inclusive.

But the John Blankenstein Foundation, which campaigns for LGBT emancipation in sport, said it had noticed declining enthusiasm for its workshops and growing numbers of parents who voiced objections.

Karin Blankenstein, who set up the organisation in 2008, told NOS: “I get the sensitivity around transgender people in sport at the top level, but this is about amateur sport.”

#2347. Big wins for PSV and Twente close gap on leaders Feyenoord

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

A late equaliser for Fortuna Sittard in De Kuip proved costly for Feyenoord as PSV and Twente racked up huge victories to move within two points of the Eredivisie leaders.

Feyenoord paid the price for failing to finish off the Limburgers, with Luciano Valente’s 59th-minute goal the only reward for their dominance.

Fortuna equalised with four minutes left when Shiloh ’t Zand, on loan from Feyenoord, was allowed to run through to the penalty area and beat Tjark Ernst with a low shot.

Feyenoord coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst made no effort to hide his disappointment. “We know that Fortuna are the kind of team who can score from the few attacks they have,” he said. “If you don’t score the goals, things like this can happen.”

Second-placed Ajax are a point behind after a narrow win at home against AZ ended an 11-match winless streak against the Alkmaar club, as Tolu Arokodare’s early header proved decisive.

Twente recorded the biggest win of the weekend with a 9-0 demolition of Excelsior in Rotterdam, with Wout Weghorst scoring four times and Sondre Ørjasaeter notching up five assists.

Excelsior collapsed following the fifth-minute dismissal of Nesto Groen for a reckless high challenge. The home side were 5-0 down at half-time, with Weghorst claiming a hat-trick in the opening 33 minutes.

PSV bagged seven goals in a one-sided encounter with bottom club Willem II, with all the goals coming from different scorers including Ricardo Pepi, who came on in the second half after a six-week absence through injury.

Telstar opened up a three-point gap at the bottom over ADO Den Haag with a 4-2 win against Utrecht in a painful return to Velsen-Zuid for Telstar’s former coach Anthony Correia.

ADO went down 1-0 in Zwolle, with PEC’s Dylan Mbayo scoring the only goal with a first-time finish on the stroke of half-time.

The match between Cambuur Leeuwarden and Go Ahead Eagles was suspended in the 82nd minute after visiting fans threw fireworks onto the pitch.

The clubs agreed with the KNVB not to play the final minutes and allow Cambuur’s 4-0 advantage to stand as the final result.

Go Ahead’s director Jan-Willem van Dop said the club would take action against those responsible: “I am ashamed on behalf a small group of supporters who ruined things for the club and a large hare of the fans in the away end,” he said.

#2342. Dual passports issue continues to plague Dutch-based footballers

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Dutch-born footballers who were called up to play for former colonies such as Suriname and Indonesia are still in limbo over their national status, six months after the “passportgate” crisis first flared up.

Suriname’s parliament passed a law in September enabling players who are selected for the national team to gain citizenship automatically.

But the Dutch immigration service IND said the law passed in Suriname made no difference to the rules on dual nationality in the Netherlands.

“The fact that Surinamese nationality has been obtained by legal means is not enough on its own to qualify for one of the exceptional categories under our law,” a spokesman for the Dutch justice ministry told broadcaster NOS.

The law was intended to settle the question of dual nationality for footballers, which arose last March when NAC Breda challenged the eligibility of Go Ahead Eagles defender Dean James.

James took Indonesian citizenship last year after accepting an invitation to play for the national team, but under Dutch dual nationality rules he was required to give up his original passport.

The Dutch football association KNVB launched an investigation into the employment status of players who were called up by countries such as Suriname, Cape Verde and Indonesia. In the meantime it arranged for Dutch-based players to be given temporary work permits so they could keep playing for their clubs.

Losing Dutch nationality also stripped them of their EU citizenship, meaning they would need to obtain visas and meet stricter criteria, such as an annual salary threshold of €600,000, to be eligible to play in the Eredivisie.

Several players have passed up invitations to join the Surinamese squad because of the uncertainty, including Danilho Doekhi of Lazio, Milan van Ewijk of Coventry City and Jayden Oosterwolde of Fenerbahçe.

National team manager Henk Fraser said it was “inexplicable” that players’ livelihoods were being damaged by their desire to play international football.

“Both countries have to get together and clarify the situation,” he told NOS. “This is a straightforward case of people following their hearts and wanting to play for Suriname, but not knowing where they stand.”

Some players said they felt misled by the Surinamese football association, which told them they were playing for the national team on “sports passports” that allowed them to remain Dutch citizens.

Former Feyenoord winger Jean-Paul Boëtius said he only discovered after he was first called up for Suriname last year that he had inadvertently renounced his Dutch citizenship as a result.

Shortly afterwards he received a letter from the city council in Rotterdam telling him to hand in his Dutch passport, which he had recently renewed, because it was no longer valid.

“I now have a sticker in my Surinamese passport that says I have a residency permit allowing me to live in the Netherlands,” he told NOS. “But that’s only for a year. It’s a very difficult situation. I want to become Dutch again. I was born and brought up here.”

#2340. UEFA Nations League: Kane inspires England to famous victory as Tuchel's men end Spain's 43-game unbeaten run

Thomas Tuchel's England ended Spain's 43-game unbeaten run with a hard-fought 3-2 win at Metropolitano Stadium. FC Bayern's striker Harry Kane opened the scoring from the penalty-kick he won before the half-time break as the 33-year-old became the only player to net in all six League A group stage games of the UEFA Nations League.

England were 3-0 ahead by the hour mark after goals from Arsenal defenders Ezri Konsa and Ben White, but Eliott Anderson's decision that led to a red card and a penalty for Spain changed the complexion of the game.

Having won the penalty-kick that was converted by Mikel Oyarzabal, Lamine Yamal assisted substitute Nico Williams for the second goal with 15 minutes left to play. However, Bayer Leverkusen's Jarell Quansah and co. produced a defensive masterclass for the rest of the game to secure a top spot finish for England.

Spain's unbeaten run ended with their last game of 2026 as the world champions suffered their first defeat since March 2024. Luis de la Fuente's side had to settle for a second-placed finish, with England having a better goal difference after avenging their defeat at Wembley by the same scoreline.

In the other game from the group, Croatia beat Czechia 2-1 thanks to goals from former Bundesliga stars Ivan Perisic and Lovro Majer. Hoffenheim's attacker Adam Hložek scored the only goal for Czechia, who are relegated to League B after losing all six games in the group.

#2338. EA SPORTS is looking for football experts for Liga NOS to join our community!

Are you the most knowledgeable football fan around? Do you want to be involved with EA SPORTS FIFA? Well, you’re in luck as the FIFA Talent Scout team is looking for people like you.

The FIFA Talent Scout community has over 6000 active members and is always looking for individuals to help improve their football database.

This is a great opportunity to put your knowledge of your club to good use, helping to ensure that the EA SPORTS football database continues to contain the most up to date and accurate team and player data for the many leagues featured in FIFA 17.

The first step into our community is as a football analyst, who can examine team and player data and offer feedback suggesting possible changes. You can contribute towards improving player appearance, player attributes, team formations and team tactics. There is no minimum or maximum expectation in terms of your contribution to the database. The key is that when you do contribute, your feedback helps to improve the authenticity of FIFA 17.

Those who stand out may be offered the chance to join our editing team and the chance to take full responsibility for a team and its squad. We are able to offer some financial compensation to editors, but in return we do require that you are a very active contributor who works to our deadlines and in line with our guidelines.

Stay in the conversation with FIFA Talent Scout by following: https://twitter.com/FIFATALENTSCOUT

#2337. Lazio, Leite sfida Doekhi: contro il Lecce possibile coppia ex Union

I due centrali hanno giocato insieme nell’amichevole a Formello; a Napoli il portoghese era stato impiegato in una posizione nuova.

Leite e Doekhi si contendono un posto nella difesa della Lazio per la partita contro il Lecce, ma Gattuso potrebbe anche schierarli insieme. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, L’assenza di Sutalo impone una scelta al tecnico, che aspetterà il rientro di Provstgaard prima di decidere la coppia centrale. Il danese ha giocato 90 minuti sabato sera contro il Portogallo: se tornerà affaticato dalla sosta, la valutazione potrebbe cambiare.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Oggi è previsto il secondo e ultimo giorno di riposo dopo l’amichevole con il Mgarr. Da domani la Lazio comincerà a preparare la gara di sabato alle 15 all’Olimpico, mentre Gattuso continua a riflettere sulle soluzioni disponibili.

Sutalo resterà fuori ancora a lungo e salterà sia il derby sia la finale di Supercoppa. Nel cuore della difesa servirà quindi spazio per un centrale utilizzato meno degli altri. Oliver è a quota 901 minuti; seguono l’ex Ajax con 597, Doekhi con 413 e Leite con 249.

Proprio l’impiego del portoghese a Napoli ha sorpreso. Ci si aspettava l’ingresso di Danilo, ma Gattuso ha scelto il mancino anche in considerazione delle caratteristiche di Hojlund. La Lazio si è così ritrovata con due centrali dal piede sinistro preferito. Per Diogo era una posizione nuova: non vi era mai stato impiegato né provato. Ha comunque risposto alla chiamata, come nelle altre occasioni avute in questa stagione.

Prima dell’infortunio alla mano, Doekhi sembrava un titolare inamovibile. Lo stop aveva poi consentito a Sutalo di salire nelle gerarchie. La decisione presa al Maradona, dove la sostituzione di un destro con un altro destro appariva la soluzione più immediata, ha riaperto il confronto tra Doekhi e Leite.

Il Lecce di Eusebio Di Francesco è un avversario capace di creare difficoltà. A Formello la sfida viene già considerata una trappola, forse per scaramanzia, forse perché è questa la convinzione nell’ambiente. La migliore difesa della Serie A è chiamata a confermarsi, ma la scelta dei centrali arriverà soltanto dopo il ritorno di Provstgaard, previsto mercoledì. Rinunciare al danese, soprannominato Iron Man, non sarebbe semplice, soprattutto con Sutalo indisponibile.

L’esperimento di Napoli ha mostrato a Gattuso di poter contare anche sui centrali meno utilizzati. Resta aperta un’altra possibilità: Doekhi e Leite insieme dal primo minuto contro i giallorossi salentini. I due ex Union Berlino hanno giocato spesso fianco a fianco negli ultimi anni, anche se in una difesa a tre. Sabato sono stati schierati insieme a Formello nell’amichevole con il Mgarr; Leite ha anche segnato, grazie a una carambola fortunata.

#2336. Les Verts renversent Rumilly Vallières

Vous avez pu suivre la rencontre en direct sur TL7, TV8 Mont-Blanc et la chaîne YouTube de l'ASSE : une équipe des Verts très remaniée en l'absence de ses 10 internationaux (Mamour Ndiaye Kévin Pédro, Sohaib Naïr, Aron Csongvaï, Adam Baallal, Augustine Boakye, Zuriko Davitashvili, Irvin Cardona, Nadir El Jamali et Marten Cris Paalberg) et des ses 4 blessés (Gautier Larsonneur, Tamar Svetlin, Paul Eymard, Elyes Dhaoui) a laborieusement renversé 2-1 Rumilly-Vallières (National 1) cet après-midi à Annecy lors du 7e tour de Coupe de France.

Sauvé par Chico Lamba (à la 50e minute) puis par le poteau (à la 52e), Sainté a concédé l'ouverture du score juste avant l'heure de jeu sur un csc de Nathan Kasia. Les Verts ont profité d'une très brève supériorité numérique (un Haut-Savoyard ayant mis trop de temps à entrer en jeu) pour égaliser à la 68e minute grâce à Ben Old, qui a repris un tir de Thierno Ballo repoussé par le gardien. Bien aidé par une sortie ratée de ce portier consécutive à un centre de Ben Old, Joao Ferreira a inscrit 3 minutes plus tard de la tête le but de la qualification au 8e tour de son équipe, qui recevra le FC Villefranche-Beaujolais (Ligue 3) dans 2 semaines.

La compo des Verts contre le GFA Rumilly Vallières : Lavallée - Ferreira, Lamba, Kasia, Old - Depalle (Le Cardinal, 46’), Bernauer, Jaber (Mendy 76’) - Ballo, Sonko (Reynaud 36’ puis Breum 61' ), Duffus.

Les protégés de Ian Cathro ont commencé le match avec leur système 4-2-4 habituel, avec Jaber en "10" en soutien de Sonko dans l'axe. L'international israélien a ensuite reculé aux côtés de Bernauer au milieu, laissant les jeunes Depalle et Sonko (puis Reynaud) former la paire des milieux offensifs censés organiser le jeu en l'absence de Breum et Boakye... Au retour des vestiaires les Verts sont passés dans un système 3-4-3 avec Le Cardinal dans l'axe de la défense et Reynaud, puis Breum, puis Mendy en milieu offensif en soutien de Ballo et Duffus.

#2335. Feyenoord facing fireworks fine after 3-0 loss to Internazionale

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord are facing a heavy fine from European football authorities after fireworks were let off during their Champions League defeat by Internazionale.

Referee Glenn Nyberg suspended the match for several minutes after flares were fired onto the roof from outside De Kuip stadium, damaging the structure. Police later made two arrests.

“It’s obviously not right and it’ll be another fine,” coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said wearily after the game, which ended in a comprehensive 3-0 defeat for his team.

One flare landed on the pitch despite the club taking measures such as hanging nets from the gantries to stop objects being thrown from the stands. “They’re letting off fireworks from outside now,” Van Bronckhorst said. “What do you do about that?”

Uefa are likely to hit Feyenoord with a fine and could order the club to close parts of the stadium for their next home matches in the competition, against Porto on November 24 and RB Leipzig on January 27.

The club also have a suspended penalty hanging over them from last October, when they were fined €60,000 after fans let off fireworks during the match against Greek club Panathinaikos.

On the pitch, Feyenoord were undone by a classic display of Italian defending and counter-attacking, as Internazionale sat back after taking an eighth-minute lead through Yann Bisseck.

Francesco Pio Esposito headed in a second early in the second half, just as Feyenoord were pushing for an equaliser, before Piotr Zielinski made sure of the win with a shot from 20 metres.

Captain Luciano Valente said the result was “more than deserved” and the visitors had shown more “quality”.

“This defeat was harder to take than the one against Barcelona,” he told Ziggo Sport. “Because we thought going in that we could get something, but we just weren’t good enough.”

Van Bronckhorst said he was not surprised by the difference in class between the teams. “They scored the first goal out of nothing,” he said. “And Inter know better what the game needs at every stage.

“Their players have a lot of experience, they’ve been in the final twice in the last four years. So we’re at a disadvantage.”

#2334. Portuguese football around the web: from Port Vale to Seixal

In a new feature PortuGOAL will be bringing you some recommended reading about Portuguese football from around the web, penned by our own network of writers or others.

We kick off by alerting you to a superb interview in the Guardian by Nick Ames with Port Vale's Portuguese manager Bruno Ribeiro. The headline writer focused on Ribeiro's relationship with José Mourinho, but it is the detail of Ribeiro's struggle to make things work at an English third-tier team of meagre resources that makes for fascinating insight.

Benfica are top of the Liga NOS and their coach Rui Vitória continues to confound the doubters who believed he would not be up to the job at the Lisbon giants. Simon Curtis takes a look at "the quiet man at the helm of a national institution". And Benfica fans, while we've got your attention, a shout out for Tom Kundert's profile of the club's world-renowned Seixal Academy for ESPN.

Talking about Portuguese football schools, none has produced more top-class players in recent decades than Sporting's Alcochete Academy, and the excellent Outside of the Boot website has a brilliantly detailed profile written by Mateus Carvalho of another possible gem for the Lions, Francisco Geraldes, who is currently on loan at Moreirense.

To finish up this initial trip around the net, we alert you to FourFourTwo's list of the 59 best Under-21 footballers from around the world. The section we highlight has a mini profile of Porto striker André Silva, who will be hoping to net his first senior international goal tonight for the Seleção against Andorra. In case you're wondering, the British magazine rank him at number 23.

#2333. UEFA Nations League: Olise and Kumbedi help France top Group A1 as Belgium see off Italy

Michael Olise and Saël Kumbedi helped France to a top spot finish in Group A1 of the UEFA Nations League. Zinedine Zidane's men scored two second-half goals to beat Türkiye 2-1 on the final matchday, while Belgium will now follow them in the quarter-finals after a 4-1 success over Italy.

Roberto Mancini's side missed out on the knockout stages as they failed to build on their emphatic victory over France. Olise had another solid performance for Les Bleus, although it was the first time he didn't register a goal or an assist under the new head coach.

Kumbedi suffered relegation with Wolfsburg last season, but Zidane called the 2. Bundesliga fullback for the senior national team after Pierre Kalulu's injury. After coming on as a substitute against Italy, the 21-year-old played the entire 90 minutes on Sunday.

The sought-after attacker Junior Eli Kroupi also played his first home game against Türkiye, and he capped it off by scoring France's second goal after Emirhan Topcu's own-goal at the start of the second-half. Can Uzun's Türkiye relegated to League B after losing all six games in the group.

Italy opened the score against Belgium in the other game from the group, but Marc van Bommel's men responded immediately as their big win sent them to the knockout stages. The last eight of the competition will take place in March with a two-legged format as Germany hope to get involved with a win over Netherlands on Monday.

#2331. Lazio, Hernanes vede in Taylor il nuovo Profeta dopo la giocata a Napoli

L’ex centrocampista ha analizzato il passaggio per Frattesi al Maradona, nato da un varco intuito tra due difensori.

Hernanes vede in Taylor un altro «Profeta» della Lazio. L’ex centrocampista brasiliano è arrivato a questa definizione riguardando le giocate dell’olandese al Maradona. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Sul suo profilo Instagram, profeta.hernanes, si è soffermato soprattutto su un passaggio per Frattesi nei primi minuti della partita: «Nella Lazio c’è un altro Profeta, questo ragazzo è in grado di vedere il futuro e di non essere ingolosito dal presente».

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Hernanes, oggi imprenditore nel settore enogastronomico e vitivinicolo, oltre che commentatore e analista tattico, ha letto in quell’azione la capacità di Taylor di anticipare uno spazio ancora da aprire. È da qui che nasce il paragone con il soprannome che lo accompagnava quando giocava.

Taylor avanzava sulla sinistra, mentre Frattesi arrivava da dietro. Più avanti, Pinamonti chiedeva il pallone in area; nelle vicinanze c’erano Rahmani e Rafa Marin. L’olandese ha scelto invece di indirizzare il passaggio tra i due difensori del Napoli, intuendo il varco nel quale Frattesi si sarebbe inserito.

Hernanes ha spiegato così la scelta: «C’era spazio per l’imbucata, ma Taylor prevede il movimento del difensore in copertura... Taylor ha visto il futuro e ha messo la palla nello spazio che non esisteva nel presente, ma nel futuro». Frattesi ha ricevuto il pallone, ma ha calibrato male il tiro. «Meraviglioso», ha concluso Hernanes nella sua analisi, ripresa dal Corriere dello Sport.

La lettura dell’ex biancoceleste richiama quella sintetizzata da Gattuso con le parole «Taylor vede cose che noi umani...»: entrambe riguardano la capacità del centrocampista di individuare in anticipo lo sviluppo dell’azione.

Con Gattuso, Taylor si muove da mezzala sia nella costruzione sia negli inserimenti offensivi. Gioca più alto o più basso secondo le fasi della partita, resta in movimento e percorre il campo da un’area all’altra. È partito titolare in tutte le prime undici gare. Nell’undicesima, contro il Napoli, ha superato gli 11 chilometri percorsi, secondo nella Lazio soltanto a Isaksen.

Ha vinto 11 contrasti, lo stesso numero di Belahyane e Floriani: è il primato di squadra. Nella parte centrale del campo, i suoi recuperi sono 23; Belahyane ne conta 48. Sono dati che descrivono il contributo di Taylor anche quando la Lazio deve riconquistare il pallone.

Nella metà campo avversaria, Taylor ha effettuato 385 passaggi, il numero più alto della squadra. Ne sono riusciti 330, due meno dei 332 completati da Belahyane. All’olandese appartiene anche il primato nelle verticalizzazioni, con 164.

Taylor è inoltre un riferimento per la catena di sinistra, ora modificata dagli stop di Tavares e Zaccagni. Gattuso ne ha sottolineato il lavoro in entrambe le fasi: «Fa funzionare bene la catena. Si abbassa e ci fa giocare bene. Gli ho fatto i complimenti perché prima non pressava così, ora lo fa con veemenza. Per noi è importantissimo. Io vedo quello che fa e dà alla squadra. Anche coi suoi tagli ci dà grandi soluzioni. A volte dobbiamo decidere se tenere lui o Nuno centralmente, dobbiamo giocare bene di catena. Sta facendo molto bene».

#2330. Contribute to help victims of the Pedrógão Grande tragedy

Forest fires occur every summer in Portugal but the tragedy that struck the community of Pedrógão Grande in the centre of the country is unprecedented. Over 60 people lost their lives, many trapped in their cars when trying to flee from the flames.

This Just Giving page is a simple way for people, wherever they are in the world, to make a contribution to help rebuild these communities and bring some comfort to those who lost everything in the blink of an eye. The money raised will be sent to a network of local authorities and charities working in the area affected. Thank you for your support.

#2329. Max toujours lucide

Capitaine de l'équipe avant l'entrée en jeu de Julien Le Cardinal, Maxime Bernauer n'aura, comme beaucoup de ses coéquipiers, pas réalisé le meilleur match de sa saison.

A la sortie du match, toujours aussi clair et lucide dans ses analyses, expliquant au micro de TL7 que ça aurait bien pu se fini aux tirs au but, il a lâché un simple et bien senti : "On a fait le minimum. Il n'y a pas de quoi fanfaronner. Ils ont été meilleurs que nous dans le jeu, il n'y a pas de doute là-dessus. Mais on a fait le plus important, on va retourner au boulot. On n'est pas fier de ce qu'on a produit, mais on est content d'être qualifiés".

#2328. PSV edged out by sharper Club Brugge in Champions League defeat

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV Eindhoven endured a frustrating night in the Champions League as their defensive frailties were exposed in a 2-1 defeat by Club Brugge.

PSV started full of attacking intent and should have taken an early lead when Ivan Perisic put Sergiño Dest clean through on goal, but the defender’s shot was too close to goalkeeper Yann Sommer.

Instead it was the visitors who took the lead with their first real attack when Nicolo Tresoldi headed in a teasing cross from Joaquin Seys.

PSV equalised early in the second half as an outstanding pass by Sven Mijnans into the path Lutsharel Geertruida allowed the defender to charge to the byline. His pullback found its way to Kodai Sano, whose shot took a deflection off Seys into the net.

But five minutes later PSV were caught out as another razor-sharp pass by Seys released Carlos Forbs in space to net the winner.

The home side refused to give up, but were unable to find a way past Sommer in the closing stages as the Belgian side closed out the win.

Team captain Mauro Junior admitted that PSV had been undone by shortcomings at the back. “We had a lot of opportunities in the first half but we didn’t score,” he told Ziggo Sport.

“We want to attack and score goals, but we weren’ well organised today in defence. We need to keep our heads up and look forward to the next game.”

The result leaves PSV in 24th place, just above the elimination line, ahead of their next match away to Real Madrid on November 24, with four points from their opening four matches.

#2326. Hertha BSC’s attacking numbers underline impressive start to 2. Bundesliga season

The Berliners have scored 25 goals so far, the joint-third highest total in the division, while their underlying numbers suggest their attacking success is built on more than simply clinical finishing.

According to Bundesliga Match Facts powered by AWS, Hertha have accumulated an expected goals (xGoals) rating of 22.9, the second-highest figure in the division. Only Heidenheim have generated a higher total, highlighting the quality of opportunities the capital club have consistently created.

Their shot efficiency rating of 2.1 also places them sixth in the division. Although several teams have been more clinical in front of goal, Hertha’s combination of regular chance creation and effective finishing has established them as one of the 2. Bundesliga’s most dangerous attacking sides.

One of the encouraging aspects of Hertha’s attacking development has been the contribution of different players throughout the squad, having relied heavily on individuals in years gone by.

Sebastian Grønning has provided an important focal point in the final third, while other members of the squad such as Oluwaseun Adewumi, Josip Brekalo and Mads Pedersen have also demonstrated their ability to contribute in attacking areas.

Rather than depending entirely on one goalscorer, Hertha have demonstrated an ability to find contributions from different areas of the pitch.

Their 5-3 victory over Eintracht Braunschweig provided an excellent example of that attacking depth. Despite falling two goals behind, Hertha responded with an impressive comeback, demonstrating their ability to create opportunities and punish opposition mistakes.

Their 2-1 victory over Wolfsburg before the break was another significant result, with Adewumi and Deyovaisio Zeefuik finding the net in front of a packed Olympiastadion. Against one of their promotion rivals, Hertha showed they could translate their attacking quality into important points.

The relationship between Hertha’s expected goals and actual goals scored is particularly interesting.

Their 22.9 xGoals figure indicates that they have consistently created opportunities worth more than 22 expected goals, while their 25 goals demonstrate that they have also managed to convert those chances effectively.

Although the difference suggests a degree of overperformance in front of goal, the gap is relatively modest compared to the overall volume of opportunities created.

This is important when assessing whether their current position is sustainable. Hertha are not simply benefiting from an unusually clinical spell; their underlying chance creation suggests they have the attacking foundations to remain competitive throughout the campaign.

Ranking second for xGoals, joint-third for goals scored and sixth for shot efficiency, they are performing strongly across all three attacking indicators.

For a club that has endured several frustrating years since dropping out of the Bundesliga in 2023, the current campaign represents a welcome change in fortunes.

Hertha have not only produced the results required to reach the summit but have also developed an attacking profile that compares favourably with the division’s strongest sides.

There is still a considerable amount of football to be played, and maintaining their current standards over an entire campaign will be the real challenge.

However, the numbers provide plenty of encouragement for supporters in the capital. With consistent chance creation, contributions from across the squad and an increasingly convincing attacking record, Hertha have established themselves as genuine contenders for promotion.

Their position at the top of 2. Bundesliga is no longer simply a reflection of a good run of results. The underlying statistics suggest there are solid reasons to believe their resurgence can continue.

#2325. Les U16 butent sur un Andrézieux valeureux

#2321. Top five football betting tips and tricks

Football is the world’s most famous sport, and it is the sport of choice of a great many sports punters as well. Especially popular in Europe, betting on football can be an incredibly profitable enterprise, so long as you know how to do it.

Getting started on football betting is far from easy, though. This form of gambling requires dedication, investment, and persistence. As you gain experience, your intuition will improve and you will be able to make more correct predictions.

But experience is not the only thing that can help you become a better football punter. In fact, you can substantially improve your betting strategy by simply following some basic football betting tips.

You might find shopping around boring and unhelpful, but you just have to do it when you search sites for football betting. As you know, the higher the odds, the bigger the payout. What you may have missed, however, is that odds are different at each bookmaker. Having said that, shopping around for the best odds is sensible, as it rewards you with bigger payouts.

In this line of thought, sticking to one bookmaker is virtually pointless. Even if the bookie of your choosing offers the best odds for a number of matches, it is highly unlikely that their odds will remain the best for long.

An obvious one, we know. And yet, you will be surprised by the number of football bettors who wager on football, but do not follow the sport. So, why is it important to follow the action closely?

First of all, by watching football matches, you can take part in the most exciting football betting market – the live betting market. What is more important, however, is that following the action provides an invaluable insight into teams and players’ style, condition, and determination – important factors  to consider when placing a bet.

As you watch more and more games, you will improve your analytic abilities. In time, you will be able to make more accurate predictions about the outcome of different matches, which, of course,  will result in placing more winning bets.

As you know, odds are based on the probability of occurrence of a certain outcome; the higher the odds, the more unlikely it is for that outcome to actually occur. But what happens when the odds offered by the bookmaker represent a probability that is lower than the actual probability of an outcome occurring? In such a situation, you can place what is called a value bet.

If you want to become a professional football punter, you should always search for value when betting, as it leads to huge profits

Favoured by beginners in particular, accumulators are a type of football wager which allows you to bet on as many fixtures as you like. Most commonly, you will be asked to choose a match winner, but as the market is diverse, you may be offered a number of other betting options. What makes accumulators an appealing betting market is the huge potential payout – here, you can stake as little as £1 and have the potential to win more than £500,000, depending on the bookmaker and the number of selected fixtures.

If you find accumulators’ payouts too good to be true, you are right. As a matter of fact, there is a reason for the odds to be set so high – actually winning an accumulator bet, especially one which involves a wide selection of matches, is nearly impossible. You see, all you need for your whole wager to lose is one wrong prediction. For example, if you bet on 10 matches and just one of them goes wrong, your entire accumulator is lost.

To make money by betting on football, avoid betting on accumulators – the risk is simply not worth it.

Finally, if you want to be a successful football punter, learn how not to mix emotions with betting. This is easier said than done, though.

Whenever your favourite team plays, you want them to win, regardless of how (im)possible that may be. If you do not learn how to leave emotions out of betting, however, you are highly likely to put money on your favourite team even if they have no chance of winning the match. Conversely, should you base your bets on hard facts and not on emotions, you increase your chances to win.

Our advice – avoid betting on fixtures that involve one of your favourite teams, that is the easiest way to keep emotions at bay.

#2319. Alphonso Davies injury diagnosis confirmed after Canada setback

Bayern Munich were dealt an injury blow last week when Alphonso Davies was forced off in the first half of Canada's 3-2 win over Guatemala.

Canada Soccer has now confirmed the nature of the injury.

"Following a medical scan today in Toronto, it has been confirmed that Alphonso Davies sustained a minor lower-body muscle strain. He is travelling back to Germany, where he will continue to receive ongoing care," the Canadian Football Association was quoted as saying by The Athletic.

Meanwhile, Bild reports that Bayern's medical staff have also confirmed the muscle strain, with the positive news being that a more serious injury has been ruled out.

According to TZ, the injury is expected to keep Davies out for 7-10 days, with Bayern hoping to have the Canada captain available for nezt week's Champions League game against Lille.

Davies himself has opened up about the setback and his recurring muscle problems in an interview with OneSoccer.

“Having had too many hamstring injuries, I know what they feel like, you know? It's not the first time. I know what they feel like,” Davies said, adding:

“I knew I felt this feeling before. And me taking myself out of the game, for me, for the team, was better than me pushing through. Because the feeling I felt, I felt this before, and I've tried to push through last time, and it didn't end well. We don't know yet how severe it is. But for me I know for sure there's something.”

Football has always been by far the biggest sport in Portugal. He currently plays for Italian giants Juventus, and despite a disappointing season for the storied club, Ronaldo was as effective as ever, scoring 36 goals and finishing 2020/21 as top scorer in Italy.

#2317. PSV back on top of Eredivisie as Feyenoord slump in Heerenveen

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV returned to the top of the Eredivisie with a thumping 5-2 win over Cambuur Leeuwarden as previous leaders Feyenoord lost a seven-goal thriller against the other Frisian club, Heerenveen.

It took just 46 seconds for PSV to get off the mark as Sven Mijnans headed in unmarked.

The midfielder added a second after 15 minutes and completed his hat-trick with a free-kick in the 80th minute to make the score 4-2, just as Cambuur were threatening a comeback.

Feyenoord’s dip in form continued as they let a two-goal lead slip at Heerenveen. The Rotterdammers looked to be in control going into the break with a trademark drilled shot by Anis Hadj Moussa and a penalty by Nacho Ferri.

But Maas Willemsen bundled in the ball from a corner on the stroke of half-time, before Ringo Meerveld came on for the second half and scored a hat-trick as Feyenoord’s defence crumbled under pressure.

“In the matches when we played well I saw a team that fought for each other,” Feyenoord coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said after the game. “Mentally strong, kept running even when we didn’t have the ball. That’s our strength. When we don’t do that we’re a lesser team.”

Mexx Meerdink scored a hotly contested late penalty to settle a tempestuous game in Alkmaar as AZ came back from 3-1 down against Groningen to claim their first win in five matches.

Goalkeeper Etienne Vaessen was given a second yellow card for protesting too hard against the decision to penalise him for upending Ayoub Oufkir. Once the commotion died down Meerdink, who had missed from the spot against Vaessen earlier in the second half, made no mistake against substitute goalkeeper Lovro Stubljar.

Ajax missed the chance to take over top spot at Twente as their former striker Wout Weghorst scored a last-minute penalty to rescue a point for the home side.

Ajax had been 2-0 up with a bullet header by their giant striker Tolu Arokodare and a long-range strike by Rayane Bounida, before an own goal by Dies Janse brought the visitors back into the game.

Fortuna Sittard also had to settle for a point against Telstar after going 2-0 up thanks to a bizarre own goal by Sem Valk. The defender’s sliced clearance looped over the head of Ronald Koeman Junior as the goalkeeper tried to head it away rather than risk being penalised for handling a backpass.

Goals just before half-time by Abdelnour Soualhia and Patrick Brouwer brought Telstar back into the game, but neither side was able to find a winner after the break.

That result allowed ADO, under the guidance of interim coach Levi Schwiebbe, to close the gap on 16th-placed Telstar to one point with a 3-1 win against Sparta, while bottom Willem II collected their third point of the season at home to PEC Zwolle.

#2314. Opinion: The morality of football

Yesterday evening, Selecao correspondent Nathan Motz accepted a unique opportunity to appear as a guest on the Moral Maze – a BBC radio broadcast dealing with matters of moral and ethical significance.

Is football a moral force for good? That was the question of the day, and each of the four guests were asked to discuss various aspects of the debate from financial fairplay to club takeovers by foreign entities to racism.

Nathan’s segment was concerned with whether football has transcendent moral value, and what that is exactly. Is there a greater significance to the game we are watching, or is it merely 22 humans kicking a leather ball into a net?

Join Nathan as he elaborates on the most significant takeaways of what was a fascinating discussion on the sport and morality in general.

Football, for some, may be the most poignant reminder of the moral universe in which we live. While debate has raged for centuries regarding the precise origin of moral absolutes and how humans ought to live, it is simply unavoidable to say humanity is often troubled by events surrounding the game we all love so dearly.

In not so distant history, every normal act of human life would have been assigned some degree of moral significance. Every action had inherently moral indicators - for good or for evil. Today, postmodern views of morality dominate. These describe morality as an entirely subjective dimension. In other words, it cannot be pinned down exactly whether behaviors are objectively good or bad. We can only find fault with someone else’s interpretation based on their own unique moral code. Morality is relative to the individual or society.

Yet last night’s dialogue concerning football and human behavior was filled with moral accusations and claims which strongly suggested humans are not content with relativist morality. Indeed, we are at times frightened by and disgusted with what we observe in and around football. Our sense of moral justice is deeply disturbed. But with respect to what exactly?

In responding to the panelist’s questions, I observed a familiar paradigm. Each time I have discussed any aspect of moral behavior, the speculation humans are good or at worst morally neutral animals continually reemerges. This widely accepted presupposition is then followed shortly by disgust at some aspect of human behavior. Almost as if to ask “why are all these good humans behaving so badly?” while ignoring the very real implications of this line of inquiry. A question rendered unanswerable by the insistent a priori assumptions from which it was derived.

If humans are intrinsically beneficent, conscientious, fair, humble, and honest then why is football rife with racism, misogyny, greed, disenfranchisement, lies, cheating, violence, and the hypocrisy to deny the occurrence of any such behaviors except when a rival club is the guilty party? Behaviors which are perpetrated by individual supporters, fanatical groups, club administrations, corporate bodies such as FIFA, political entities, nations, and even the players themselves. No one participating faction is guiltless.

To so blithely sidestep more sufficient explanations for the empirical reality of wickedness in football and in human behavior more generally is not just factually incorrect. It undermines real attempts to understand the root of these behaviors where they might be dealt with more effectively.

To listen to The Moral Maze episode entitled “Football: a moral force for good?” produced by BBC radio and featuring PortuGOAL’s Seleção correspondent Nathan Motz click on this link

Furthermore, in good faith, how successful is a response against heinous evil when those perpetrating it are regarded as behaving in accord with their unique moral ambitions? Humans refuse to accept their indisputably objective moral lacking at a fundamental level. This restricts us from ever doing any more than “stamping out” the alleged impropriety of others – while we ourselves may readily engage in these very same behaviors. Moral relativism cannot account for the moral outrage emanating from all corners of the football world. Is Qatar wrong to perpetrate human rights atrocities or is it allowable because their culture is indifferent? Is hooliganism objectively wrong, or only when someone else’s club is responsible?

The overlooking of our own failings while lashing out at others for theirs is quintessential to the morally fallen nature of humanity itself. Football tells all. There are not good people and bad people. There are only people who both willfully and unintentionally transgress the moral code embedded in each of our hearts. A moral law which can only have ever been given by a moral Lawgiver. Otherwise, it is truly your word against mine as it pertains to the moral validity of all behaviors - even those which may deeply violate your conscience.

Football generates an episodic, semantic, and imagistic description of universal archetypes: good and evil, sacrifice and greed, cruelty and mercy, hero and anti-hero. It speaks to every arc of life’s plotline: tragedy, comedy, victory, defeat, the finite nature of our circumstances. It is one of the preeminent existential dialects alongside religion, visual art, written and spoken language, music, and theatre. Football plucks a primal chord.

As metaphysics have been curtailed to just physics in the modern secular West, football has only elevated our awareness of the often dire moral predicaments of our species. Yet it is clear the moral conversation does not originate on a football pitch. The pitch is but a grand stage upon which moral themes may be observed and critiqued, a mirror held in front of our own soul.

The desire for ultimate purpose, for meaning beyond the material realm, never disappeared in modernity. Arguably, it has only become more desperate as the outcry for ultimate significance comes under more scrutiny and criticism by those who constrain ontological explanations to those accounted for by physics. The severity of the mental health crisis in the West is in no small part explained by the terrifying existential awareness we are cut adrift in an abstract sea of meaningless, hopeless wandering between two oblivions - that before mortal life began and that at mortal life’s end. Let football be one voice which reassures you - there is ultimate meaning and good reasons why football so unsettles our craving for justice.

In a palpable, unavoidable way, football and supporter communities across Europe are encountering moral quandaries. In Portugal, age-old debates between FC Porto and Benfica have routinely been haunted by, in many cases, farcical and cynical hypocrisy. Throughout its history, the FPF has been susceptible to showing partiality to a club, player, or style which ultimately degrades the performance of our beloved Seleção. Deny it all we like, and not one aspect of this reality is changed.

In conclusion, whatever your thoughts or beliefs on the subject of moral significance, its origin, the meaning of life, and how football draws that out, I hope it will be beneficial to examine these matters more deeply. In my zeal, I admit I have not always been prudent to initiate these discussions in a way which respects thoughtful disagreement. It is my hope this effort to bring that discussion to Portuguese football will be well-received and carefully pondered. But I will contentedly listen to those followers who may be genuinely unhappy or unsupportive of this or any other idea about which I write.

The readers of this website and the larger Portuguese football loving world are my people. It is a joy and a responsibility I take seriously whenever I have the opportunity to raise important questions about the Selecao, the game, and our lives in general.

#2312. Nous n’allons pas penser à ce match très longtemps

Le médiocre match que les Verts ont joué cet après-midi contre Rumilly Vallières finira vite aux oubliettes, sauf si les protégés de Ian Cathro ont la bonne idée de soulever la Coupe de France le 15 mai 2027 au Stade de France, 50 ans après le dernier sacre stéphanois dans cette compétion. On se souviendra alors que leur épopée aura démarré lors de ce piètre match à Annecy.

L'entraîneur des Verts ne s'est pas trop étendu en Haute-Savoie après la laborieuse qualification de son équipe très remaniée. Il a surtout expliqué pourquoi son habituel capitaine et son meneur de jeu danois ont démarré cette rencontre sur le banc malgré les très nombreuses absences (10 internationaux présentement en sélection + 4 joueurs à l'infirmerie).

"Notre travail et notre responsabilité étaient de passer au tour suivant. On l’a fait. Mais je suis très déçu de notre première période où nous ne sommes pas parvenus à montrer le niveau que nous voulons avoir, en manquant des choses simples. On a réussi à être plus connectés en seconde période après des changements, avec des erreurs malgré tout. On a produit des choses plus positives, ce qui a fait que nous avons aussi concédé plus de situations avec certains moments de flottement. Honnêtement, nous n’allons pas penser à ce match très longtemps.  Julien Le Cardinal et Jakob Breum n'ont pas démarré la rencontre car ils ont beaucoup joué depuis le début de saison. L’objectif était de gérer leur charge. Jakob a notamment quelques gênes que nous devons prendre en compte. Dans un scénario idéal, ils seraient restés sur le banc. Mais on devait faire le nécessaire pour se qualifier et on s'est appuyés sur eux."

#2311. Johan Cruyff’s grandson called up to play for USA under-18 team

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The most famous name in Dutch football could appear on an international men’s team sheet for the first time in 30 years after Johan Cruyff’s grandson was called up for the United States’ upcoming matches in Europe.

Shae Cruijff-Beane, the son of Johan Cruyff’s daughter Chantal and her American husband Todd Beane, was included in the squad that will play against Norway and Portugal in the next seven days.

Cruijff-Beane plays as a midfielder for Gimnàstic de Tarragona, a club in the Spanish third tier based near Barcelona. His older brother Jordan also came through the club’s ranks and is currently playing US college football at Westmont College in California.

Shae, 17, is eligible to play for the USA through his father and Spain and the Netherlands through his mother, who spent part of her childhood in Catalonia while Johan Cruyff was playing for Barcelona in the 1970s.

The USA under-18 squad also includes an Ajax player, midfielder Xander Newstead, who grew up in San Francisco and joined the Amsterdam club’s youth team set-up at the age of 10, beforec signing professional terms last year.

Johan Cruyff went on to play in the United States for Los Angeles Aztecs and Washington Diplomats before returning to the Netherlands, winding down his career at Ajax and Feyenoord.

His son Jordi played for Barcelona and Manchester United and won nine international caps in 1996. Last year he was appointed technical director at Ajax, the club where his father began his career.

#2309. “I think I helped him win the Ballon d'Or" – Luka Vušković makes cheeky Harry Kane admission

After England's 2-1 win over Croatia last week, Harry Kane revealed that he had been keeping an eye on Luka Vušković in the Premier League and praised the young centre-back.

Vušković, who faced Kane and Bayern Munich while on loan at Hamburger SV last season, was asked about the England captain's comments by Croatian outlet HRT Sport – and came up with a humorous response.

“It means a lot to me. I think I helped him win the Ballon d'Or too, because he scored about 10 goals against me,” he said with a laugh.

“I'm very glad he won that award because he had an incredible season and deserved it. I'm delighted he said that about me.”

In reality, Kane scored three goals against HSV last season – netting twice in Bayern's 5-0 victory in September 2025 before scoring once more in a 2-2 draw in January 2026.

#2307. Du Baume au coeur de l'élimination de Rumilly Vallières

Pas exempt de tout reproche sur le but qui a éliminé son équipe hier à Annecy, le gardien du GFA Rumilly Vallières Valentin Baume revient sur la victoire des Verts dans la dernière édition du Dauphiné Libéré. Extraits.

"En seconde période, on est rentré sur le terrain avec beaucoup d’ambitions. On voulait s’installer chez eux et aller les chercher pour produire notre jeu. Cela a payé car on parvient à ouvrir le score. Malheureusement, on prend ces deux buts derrière et on perd le match alors qu’on aurait vraiment pu réaliser un exploit.

Les Verts égalisent à 11 contre 10, je n’ai pas trop compris ce qu’il s’est passé au moment du changement. Au final, ils ont une situation où je me jette pour réaliser l’arrêt miracle [devant Thierno Ballo, ndp2]. Je parviens à toucher le ballon, malheureusement, il revient sur un autre joueur de Saint-Etienne [Ben Old, ndp2] qui marque.

Sur le deuxième but, il centre fort. J’essaye de sortir pour éloigner le ballon le plus loin possible. Malheureusement, je suis un peu court, je ne le repousse pas assez fort et il retombe sur la tête de leur joueur [Joao Ferreira, ndp2] qui marque. Je suis un peu malchanceux et malheureux sur cette situation."

#2306. Zirkzee fires Netherlands to win over Serbia

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 8 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 6 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 5 of the Eredivisie ...

Is AZ Alkmaar's academy now the best in the Netherlands? ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 4 of the Eredivisie ...

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul react to the Netherlands' Nations League ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul preview the Netherlands' Nations League game ...

Netherlands edged out Serbia 2-1 in Eindhoven to move top of their Nations League group.

Xavi decided to name Teun Koopmeiners in the starting eleven but at centre-back alongside Virgil van Dijk, who was earning his 100th cap. Ryan Gravenberch, Tijjani Reijnders and Noa Lang all came into the eleven.

Netherlands made a strong start and missed two good chances to take the lead within the first two minutes. They then dominated possession but it took until the 31st minute for Oranje to take the lead. Koopmeiners free kick evaded everyone and nestled in the corner.

Oranje were only ahead for four minutes as Mihaijo Cvetkovic was allowed far too much space in the Netherlands box to loop in a header. It could have been worse for the Netherlands before the break but Pavlovic squandered a chance when in on goal.

Xavi brought on Joshua Zirkzee for Mexx Meerdink at the break but the best chances early in the second half were for Serbia. Cvetkovic should have put the visitors ahead.

Xavi turned to the bench again with Gjivai Zechiel, Ruben van Bommel and Jorrel Hato all appearing. Reijnders should have made it 2-1 but when through on goal he scooped the ball over the bar.

Netherlands retook the lead after 72 minutes with Denzel Dumfries crossing for Reijnders to square it to an unmarked Zirkzee, who tapped in. It should have been 3-1 straight away but Reijnders was denied through on goal.

The Netherlands sat back and that allowed Serbia to put on pressure. That pressure increased when Micky van de Ven was forced off and Oranje played the last five minutes with only ten men.

Bart Verbruggen made a big save to deny Nemanja Gudelj and Netherlands managed to hold on for the three points.

Netherlands first four games under Xavi see them win two and draw two. They will finish the Nations League at home to Germany and then away to Greece.

FT Netherlands 2-1 SerbiaIt’s three points but far from convincing in the end. Xavi remains unbeaten in his first four games in charge

Netherlands U21’s have one foot in the European Championships after a 3-0 win in Denmark in the first leg of their playoff.

After finishing second in their group, the Netherlands need to overcome Denmark in a two-legged playoff to seal their spot in the Euros.

Michael Reiziger handed debuts to Aaron Bouwman and Don-Angelo Konadu, while Kees Smit was not fit enough to take part.

Two own goals from Tobias Storm and Lucas Høgsberg handed the Netherlands an early 2-0 lead and just before half time, Thom van Bergen added a third.

In the second half, the Netherlands eased to victory and they will now look to seal the win on Tuesday in the Netherlands.

#2297. Brilliant Netherlands Put Seven past Greece

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 8 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 6 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 5 of the Eredivisie ...

Is AZ Alkmaar's academy now the best in the Netherlands? ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 4 of the Eredivisie ...

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul react to the Netherlands' Nations League ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul preview the Netherlands' Nations League game ...

The Netherlands were in excellent form on Friday as they hammered Greece 7-0 to all but seal their spot in the Nations League Quarter-finals.

Xavi had Frenkie de Jong back in the midfield as he partnered Joey Veerman in midfield. Jurrien Timber played left-back as Micky van de Ven replaced the missing Virgil van Dijk. Up front, Donyell Malen led the line.

The first half was a joy to behold for Oranje fans as Netherlands ran rampant with five goals. Crysencio Summerville got the party started with a fine finish in the sixth minute. Veerman then sent an excellent through ball for Tijjani Reijnders to make it 2-0.

The Netherlands were playing lovely football with Veerman, Timber and Malen all involved. It was quickly 3-0 as Reijnders tapped in before the midfielder completed his hattrick in the 33rd minute. Before the break, Timber added the fifth.

In the second half, Teun Koopmeiners replaced De Jong but the Netherlands kept pushing forward and Timber got his second to make it 6-0. Malen then got amongst the goals for the seventh on the hour mark.

Xavi then rang more changes with Ruben van Bommel, Noa Lang, Gjivai Zechiel and Ian Maatsen all coming on. The changes took away some of the momentum and there was no further goals before the end.

The Netherlands are now almost guaranteed a place in the quarter-finals and only a huge loss to Germany on Monday would prevent this.

A wonderful evening for the Netherlands.7-0 winners over Greece!Reijnders, Veerman, Malen, Timber and Summerville all excellent.

#2296. Mandas, stipendi e rinnovo: la Lazio valuta un adeguamento, restano gli attriti

Il portiere non intende per ora estendere il contratto al 2029; in passato rinunciò a quattro mensilità.

Mandas rinunciò a quattro mensilità per tentare l’esperienza in Premier League. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Quel precedente, legato al caso stipendi, pesa oggi sul rapporto contrattuale con la Lazio: il club potrebbe offrirgli un adeguamento, ma al momento il portiere non intende prolungare l’accordo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Al momento della partenza, Mandas dovette accettare la richiesta economica di Lotito e rinunciare a quattro stipendi. Il Corriere dello Sport ricorda quell’episodio tra le mosse del presidente biancoceleste in occasione delle partenze.

Il portiere viene inoltre accostato a percentuali definite da record. La possibilità di un adeguamento economico, tuttavia, si inserisce in un rapporto segnato da quanto accaduto nell’ultima stagione.

Mandas aveva rinnovato nel 2025 e il suo accordo scade nel 2029. Per la Lazio significa avere una copertura contrattuale per altri due anni e mezzo, senza però poter contare, allo stato attuale, sulla disponibilità del portiere a spostare più avanti la scadenza.

Un adeguamento-premio rientra tra le possibili intenzioni della società, ma le chance di una nuova intesa sono ora limitate. Non c’è soltanto l’aspetto economico: incidono anche gli attriti e i precedenti dell’ultima stagione.

#2295. Bayern reportedly put staggering €300m price tag on Michael Olise amid Real Madrid links

Michael Olise was linked with a move to Real Madrid last summer, but ultimately remained at Bayern Munich, who repeatedly made it clear that the Frenchman was not for sale.

That stance is unlikely to have changed, with Bayern's management remaining relaxed about Olise's future, given that he's under contract until 2029.

However, if the Frenchman ultimately decides against signing a new deal with Bayern, the German champions may have to consider selling him at some point.

According to Spanish outlet AS, should that situation arise, the Bavarians would demand as much as €300 million for their prized asset.

The 24-year-old is currently valued at €180 million by Transfermarkt.

#2294. Les anciens Verts en Coupe de France (7ème tour)

Nous avons déjà évoqué le 7ème tour de la Coupe de France avec un panorama des clubs de Ligue 2. Aux surprises que nous avions évoquées (Dijon, Rodez, Boulogne, Dunkerque, Guingamp) s'en est venue ajouter une autre, à savoir le FC Metz, éliminé hier par le club de R1, Saint-Dié SR Kellermann (1-0). Dimanche 8 novembre 2026, Crystal Palace a concédé un nouveau revers à domicile lors de la 10e journée de Premier League en s'inclinant face à Liverpool (1-3) à Selhurst Park. Menés après des buts signés Alexis Mac Allister (40e), Florian Wirtz (54e) et Alexander Isak (89e), les Londoniens ont réagi trop tard grâce à un penalty transformé par Eddie Nketiah (90e+5). Cette défaite face aux Reds constitue le septième revers en dix journées de championnat pour Crystal Palace, qui n'a remporté que deux matches depuis le début de saison. Une situation critique. Le club londonien glisse ainsi à la 20e et dernière place du classement et se retrouve lanterne rouge pour la première fois depuis près de neuf ans, en décembre 2017.

La direction de Crystal Palace aurait déjà acté la décision de limoger Pierre Sage... et cela avant même la rencontre de dimanche soir. Interrogé sur son avenir, l'entraîneur français a livré son analyse en conférence de presse d'après-match :

Pierre Sage avait signé à Crystal Palace à la mi-juin 2026 pour un contrat de trois ans en provenance du RC Lens afin de remplacer Oliver Glasner. Le bilan global du technicien français à la tête des Eagles s'avère toutefois bien insuffisant, avec 5 victoires, 3 nuls et 8 défaites en 16 matches disputés toutes compétitions confondues. En championnat, le constat comptable est particulièrement alarmant. Crystal Palace possède en effet la plus mauvaise défense de Premier League, avec un total de 24 buts concédés en dix rencontres seulement. Un secteur défensif aux abois.

D'ailleurs, l'alerte avait déjà été donnée en interne en octobre, après des défaites à Lyon et surtout une déroute à Brighton (0-5). À cela s'ajoutent des crispations palpables dans le vestiaire londonien, alimentées notamment par les mises à l'écart notables de Canvot et d'Axel Disasi.

Face à cette impasse sportive, les décideurs londoniens s'activent pour trouver un successeur sur le banc. Le Danois Thomas Frank, ancien entraîneur de Brentford et libre depuis son éviction de Tottenham en février dernier, apparaît désormais comme le grand favori pour succéder à Pierre Sage, d'après les informations de RMC Sport. Un technicien reconnu en Angleterre pour relancer la machine londonienne.

Toutefois, d'autres profils sont également cités par Sky Sports pour s'installer sur le banc de Crystal Palace. Parmi les pistes envisagées figurent notamment Frank Lampard, Sean Dyche, Vitor Pereira, Kim Hellberg, Vitor Matos et Tony Popovic. Le calendrier impose en tout cas une réaction rapide pour le club de Selhurst Park, dont le prochain match en Premier League est fixé au samedi 21 novembre 2026 sur le terrain de Coventry City.

#2292. « Je suis candidat à l'élection présidentielle de 2027 » : L'annonce inattendue de Teddy Riner !

C'est une annonce pour le moins inattendue dans le monde du sport tricolore. Ce lundi 9 novembre 2026, Teddy Riner a officialisé sa candidature à l'élection présidentielle de 2027 dans une vidéo publiée sur son compte X, tout en lançant son propre mouvement politique intitulé « La France qu'on veut ».

Le judoka français de 37 ans se lance ainsi en politique après avoir dominé le judo international. Quintuple champion olympique et onze fois champion du monde, Teddy Riner a bâti un palmarès exceptionnel qui le place au sommet de sa discipline. Alors que son avenir sportif restait au cœur des discussions depuis les Jeux de Paris, le Français surprend en ouvrant un tout autre chapitre personnel. Un virage spectaculaire.

Dans une vidéo publiée ce lundi 9 novembre 2026 sur son compte X, Teddy Riner a pris la parole face caméra afin de lever le voile sur ses intentions pour la prochaine présidentielle. Le judoka de 37 ans a officialisé sa démarche avec des mots simples et directs.

Conscient des réactions que son entrée en politique peut susciter, le quintuple champion olympique a tenu à devancer immédiatement les critiques concernant son manque d'expérience dans les fonctions publiques. Teddy Riner a ainsi déclaré : « Je sais ce qu'on va dire de moi. Il est fou, il n'est pas du sérail, il n'a pas les codes, il n'a jamais fait de politique. Mais posez-vous la question avec moi, qu'est-ce qui est fou aujourd'hui ? Je n'ai pas passé ma vie derrière un public, mais le combat a toujours fait partie de moi. » Une détermination affichée.

Pour concrétiser ses ambitions, le judoka français de 37 ans a également fondé sa propre formation politique ce lundi 9 novembre 2026, baptisée « La France qu'on veut ». Cette prise de parole sur les réseaux sociaux a d'ailleurs été accompagnée d'un entretien accordé au quotidien Le Parisien pour préciser le sens de sa démarche. Dans cet échange, Teddy Riner revendique une volonté de rassemblement sans affiliation partisane, affirmant vouloir devenir un « président qui fédère » sans se situer à droite ou à gauche.

En effet, le champion tricolore a expliqué que sa décision avait mûri dans le sillage direct de l'élan collectif suscité par les Jeux olympiques de Paris 2024. Porté par cette dynamique populaire, il entend désormais transposer cette ferveur sur le plan politique. Ainsi, son mouvement politique refuse d'entrer dans les clivages traditionnels pour privilégier une adhésion commune à l'échelle du pays. Un projet ambitieux.

Toutefois, cette ambition politique ne pousse pas Teddy Riner vers la retraite sportive. Sur le plan des compétitions, sa dernière apparition individuelle remonte aux Jeux olympiques de Paris en août 2024, suivie d'une participation sous les couleurs du PSG Judo en Ligue des champions en décembre 2024. Malgré cette nouvelle démarche politique, le quintuple champion olympique et onze fois champion du monde de judo demeure bel et bien un athlète en activité.

Au-delà de l'élection présidentielle de 2027, Teddy Riner a ainsi fait part de son souhait de participer aux Jeux olympiques de Los Angeles 2028 si ses futures responsabilités et les Français le lui permettent. Le judoka français lie donc son avenir sportif à la suite de son engagement public, avec en ligne de mire les Jeux olympiques de Los Angeles 2028.

#2291. Mamadou Coulibaly (AS Monaco) s'excuse après son geste polémique

Auteur de son premier but dans l'élite lors du match nul sur le terrain sarthois, Mamadou Coulibaly a suscité une vive polémique en mimant un geste d'égorgement. Le jeune milieu de l'AS Monaco se retrouve désormais sous la menace directe d'une lourde suspension.

Dimanche 8 novembre 2026, l'AS Monaco a été accrochée sur la pelouse du Mans FC (1-1) pour le compte de la 10e journée de Ligue 1. Si le club du Rocher espérait engranger des points précieux, la rencontre a été marquée par le geste de son joueur de 22 ans.

Titularisé dans l'entrejeu monégasque, Mamadou Coulibaly pensait passer une bonne soirée après avoir ouvert le score à la 40e minute de jeu. Mais la joie d'inscrire son tout premier but en Ligue 1 a rapidement viré au malaise lors de sa célébration. Le milieu monégasque s'est en effet dirigé vers le poteau de corner pour glisser sur les genoux, avant de tirer la langue et de mimer un geste d'égorgement avec son pouce sous la gorge. Un geste polémique.

Ce geste n'est malheureusement pas inédit en Principauté cette saison. En effet, cette célébration reproduit à l'identique celle effectuée par son partenaire de club Paris Brunner face à Strasbourg en septembre dernier, lors de la 4e journée de Ligue 1. Pour ce geste, le jeune attaquant avait été sanctionné par la commission de discipline de la LFP de quatre matches de suspension, dont deux fermes et deux avec sursis.

D'ailleurs, cette récidive au sein du même effectif met le joueur dans une situation extrêmement délicate. En raison de ce précédent disciplinaire très récent, Mamadou Coulibaly risque une sanction similaire d'au moins deux matches de suspension ferme devant la commission de discipline de la LFP. L'instance disciplinaire devrait se saisir rapidement du dossier pour trancher sur ce comportement. Un coup dur en perspective.

Conscient des remous provoqués par son geste, le joueur de 22 ans s'est exprimé devant les journalistes à l'issue de la rencontre. « Je tiens à m’excuser parce que j’ai cru comprendre que ça avait été mal compris. C’est un geste que j’ai fait par rapport un ami à moi qui m’a conseillé au sujet de la célébration. Je tiens à m'excuser si ça a pu heurter des gens. Maintenant, ce sont des choses qui se font dans d'autres championnats, je n'ai pas pensé que ça pourrait avoir des conséquences négatives », a ainsi déclaré Mamadou Coulibaly en zone mixte au micro des médias après Le Mans-Monaco.

Toutefois, cette justification risque de ne pas suffire auprès de son entraîneur Filipe Luis. Le coach monégasque avait pourtant publiquement averti son groupe en septembre après l'affaire similaire, insistant sur l'exemplarité attendue : « Il a fait une erreur. Il l'a compris tout de suite. Il s'est excusé. Le club prend cette affaire très au sérieux (…). On espère que ça ne se reproduira plus », avait prévenu le technicien en conférence de presse. Au-delà de cette polémique extra-sportive, l'AS Monaco accueillera l'AJ Auxerre au stade Louis-II le samedi 21 novembre 2026 à 20h45 pour la 11e journée de Ligue 1.

Une trajectoire fulgurante pour le latéral droit évoluant en deuxième division allemande.

En effet, Pierre Kalulu a dû déclarer forfait en raison d'une lésion aux ischios du membre inférieur gauche contractée ce week-end avec la Juventus. Comptant déjà neuf sélections avec les Bleuets, le défenseur franchit donc un cap décisif. Un choix fort.

Au total, le défenseur a disputé 62 matches sous le maillot des Gones, dont quatre apparitions sur la scène européenne en Ligue Europa lors de l'exercice 2024-2025.

Prêté au VfL Wolfsburg à l'été 2025, le latéral a ensuite été transféré définitivement le 1er janvier 2026 pour un montant de six millions d'euros. Toutefois, l'institution allemande, championne de Bundesliga en 2009 et installée dans l'élite sans discontinuer depuis 1997, a été reléguée en mai 2026 lors d'un barrage contre Paderborn (0-0, 2-1 a.p.). Un coup dur pour la formation allemande. Resté fidèle au club en 2. Bundesliga, Kumbedi est rapidement devenu un titulaire indiscutable sous les ordres de Tobias Strobl, malgré une blessure musculaire lui ayant fait manquer trois matches.

Cette convocation inattendue concrétise la passerelle directe établie entre la sélection A et les moins de 21 ans tricolores. Présent chez les Espoirs, le défenseur Nathan Zézé avait d'ailleurs rapporté le discours tenu par Zinédine Zidane :

Le parcours de Saël Kumbedi en équipe de France démarre ainsi dans ce contexte de renouvellement. Les Bleus disputeront deux matches de Ligue des nations capitaux au cours de cette trêve internationale. Ils commenceront par un déplacement face à l'Italie à Milan, programmé le jeudi 12 novembre 2026 à 20h45. L'équipe de France recevra ensuite la Turquie à Bordeaux, le dimanche 15 novembre 2026 à 20h45.
